ParlaMint-DK_20170509131446	Thank you. Monday last week it was 1 May, and it was a very good day for the Danish working class, because all parties here in the Danish Parliament were falling over each other to call themselves a working party. As early as Wednesday last week, when we had to deal with the proposal from the Enhedslisten that we are going to vote on here, it turned out that only the SF and Enhedslist supported the proposal. What's this about? Yes, it's all about these senior days, namely that it was agreed during the renewal of the agreement that 32 senior days can be saved in the last 3 years up to the retirement age. That's a really good idea. The only problem has been that if you use the pension payment to finance these days off, it will lead to a set-off in the early retirement premium. And at worst this could mean that if you take advantage of almost all the days off, it will cost twice the pre-retirement premium, i.e. more than DKK 26,000. We wanted to help to rectify this, and that is why we tabled this motion for a resolution which was then before the Committee on Employment and Social Affairs on Wednesday morning. Unfortunately, however, we had to see that only the SF supported it. The minister rejected it. He said he didn't plan on doing that. He would like at a later stage to call the parties and talk about the issue and all sorts of others who had to deal with interplay problems in relation to pensions, but here and now he did not want to solve the problem. There, of course, you can say, like the Minister, that this is not a problem he has created, because he is quite right about that. One might also say that we were some who were not aware that it was a rule that was made already in 2012. The Minister is quite right about that too. We should have been aware of that then and tried to correct it. The only problem is that it does not solve the challenge we have been given, which in practice will mean that people saving up for the pre-retirement premium will probably estimate that it will be so expensive to keep the senior days off that they do not. And that is why we are all in favour of a common intention that everyone has, namely that it should then be as advantageous as possible for seniors to remain in the labour market. Unfortunately, I also had to see to my disappointment and astonishment that the Social Democratic Party would not support the proposal either, so perhaps one should just conclude that the fact that you are a working party is something that appears in action, for example. In a moment, when we are going to vote on this proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509132222	The debate is open. This is Mrs Trine Bramsen, the Social Democratic Party. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509132231	It is quite unreasonable when criminal bikers and gangs think they have to rule and decide in our society; when they pervert the law over and over again when they threaten homeless people, blackmail businessmen, shoot in the streets and create insecurity. Unfortunately, last year we saw a large increase in the number of shootings in the biker and gang environments in Odense, in the Copenhagen area, in South Zealand and in several other places. We saw the conflicts between the groupings escalating, and we saw how ordinary Danes experienced insecurity. An innocent was even hit by gunshots, several bikers and gang members lost their lives. Of course, that's not gonna work. Therefore, at the end of 2016 we had a question debate here in the Parliamentary Chamber, which ended with a vote. It was a decision which instructed the government to call for negotiations on a new biker and gang package. That is the background to the draft law that we are discussing in the Parliamentary Chamber here today. The bill thus implements parts of the political agreement reached between the government, the Danish People's Party and the Social Democratic Party on 24 March 2017, about what is popularly called gang package III. The bill contains several amendments to the criminal code, the law on the enforcement of penalties and the law on the prohibition of residence in certain properties and a number of other matters. In particular, I would like to highlight the changes to the rules on parole for persons convicted under Article 81a of the Criminal Code. This will mean that bikers and gang criminals cannot be paroled unless they choose to go on exits. We are also tightening up the penalties for participating in biker and gang-related conflicts and for using firearms in public spaces. As I and Social Democratic rapporteurs have said over and over again before me, punishment can never stand alone. There is no point in raising the penalties to infinity if the police's tools do not go along at the same time. The bill here will make it easier for the authorities to monitor and intercept telephone calls and letters to and from an inmate that the police consider to have a leading role in the biker and gang environment. In Odense we have seen that blackmail and threats have become part of the methods of criminal groups. Many have heard about how both homeless, vulnerable and traders such as tattooists have been blackmailed. This is obviously not fair or acceptable, and that is why we are increasing the penalties for systematic blackmail, such as the death penalty. in the case of a claim for protection. In this context, I would like to make it clear that, in addition to the proposals we are discussing here today, we also need to help the victims better. That is why it is the Socialist call for us to start work on a sacrificial package to ensure much better protection for the witnesses and, above all, help for them. I should like to mention in particular that we in the Socialist Group are very concerned about preventing crime. These are good, solid tools that we are adopting here. They're necessary, and they're right. However, prevention is and remains the best investment, and that is why we are looking forward to the negotiations on a new prevention effort. With the two clear calls to the Minister for Justice and to the other parties of the Danish Parliament for a sacrificial package and a new prevention strategy, I have to say that we in the Social Democratic Party support this proposal.

We're gonna increase the penalty by 33 percent. We have nothing left for bikers and gang members who terrorize their local environment; who beat vulnerable people to their cash assistance at the beginning of the month; who blackmail tattooers and are otherwise involved in deep criminal activities. We also want to do what works, what has an effect, and what enables us to combat organised crime, while defending our fundamental freedoms in a democratic society. After all, this bill implements parts of the political agreement concluded between the government, the Danish People's Party and the Social Democratic Party, the so-called gang package III, and the bill contains several elements. The first element that we have already discussed a little is changes in the rules on parole for persons convicted under section 81a of the Criminal Code. There are several consultation responses that mention problems related to that part of the bill. Firstly, it is stated that it is unclear what is really meant by an exit programme. What is it anyway? The National Association of Municipalities notes that there is a need to clarify and define what an exit programme entails and to clarify when the participant has fulfilled the requirement to participate. For as Mr. Preben Bang Henriksen was up to say: Well how hard can it be to participate in the exit program? Yes, it may not be, if it can be defined in a hundred and seventeen different ways, it just is not clear exactly how an exit program should be understood, with the bill that lies here. The Legal Policy Association notes that a requirement for participation in the exit programme may be reasonable in many, but probably not in all cases, and they believe that there is rubber band in this and that it is legally questionable. In their reply to the hearing, the Lawyers' Council states that it is unfortunate, in relation to the provisions on participation in the exit programme, that there is legislation on intrusive consequences for infringement of some rules whose content is currently in force. is unknown or uncertain and must be regulated by an order which, by its very nature, can not enter into force at the earliest in connection with the law. So: Embossed by uncertainty combined with the fact that reducing the possibility of access to parole everything else equal will lead to fewer tools in resocializing work, and that again everything else equal will lead to more and not less crime. And it's interesting that you make a gang package that looks up that now you have to go hard to bikers and gangs and that it's the Justice Minister's iron fist that's going to get started here, but then you're doing something in this bill that simply means that resocializing work gets worse conditions and it's going to cause more crime. Then there is a whole series of sharpenings. There is an amendment to Article 81a of the Penal Code, which extends the provision to offences suitable for causing a conflict between groups of persons using firearms in the context of the conflict. So the authority to provoke a conflict is very unclear what exactly it will be in practice, but it may in fact reflect that this paragraph has been used incredibly little and in very few cases in recent years. The penalty for using firearms in public space will be set up. There is an increase in the penalty for systematic blackmail. None of those things make sense. Everyone can tell themselves that you should not shoot in public space, that you should not blackmail, but where is the proven effect that this will have a preventive effect on the fight against crime? It's nowhere. And the research results, which are also from the Ministry of Justice's Research Office, point in that direction too. It may sound awesome to say punishment increases, penalty increases, but there is no proven effect that punishment with punishment leads to less crime. One looks as though one is able to act, but it will not have an effect in the real world. At Enhedslisten we have the feeling that we would like to discuss higher penalties, we would like to do so, but we would also like to see that there is a preventive effect, that the punishments lead to less crime, and that evidence is simply absent.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509141600	Thank you to the rapporteur. Then it's the Minister of Justice. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509141614	Thank you for that and, first of all, thank you for many good and positive contributions to the legislative proposal we have here today. In the past year, we have seen an increase in the raison d'être of biker gang crime in Denmark. This has led to many violent conflicts, and the number of biker-gang-related shootings in the public space was more than tripled from 2015 to 2016. Repeatedly, the lives and health of ordinary and law - abiding citizens have been put at risk. This should not be the case in Denmark, and I am therefore proud to have entered into a very important political agreement with the Danish People's Party and the Social Democratic Party on strengthening efforts to combat biker gang crime. It is an agreement that is strongly opposed to it, and it is the political agreement that we are implementing much of today's legislative proposal. We should not accept that bikers and gangs of brutal and ruthless crime create insecurity in the streets. The Danes must be able to walk the streets without fear of being caught in the shootings of bikers and gang groups. Therefore, from the beginning of my time as Minister of Justice, it has been a top priority to sharpen the course towards bikers and gang members. Specifically, it is proposed that persons convicted under the criminal code should only be eligible for parole if they have actively and satisfactorily participated in an exit or similar programme established by the criminal justice system. At the same time, it is proposed that participation in the programme should continue during a possible parole, and otherwise it is as a clear starting point back in prison. The proposed scheme implies that the person concerned should have a real intention to leave the relevant bickering or gang grouping. I think that makes a great deal of sense, because we should not parole inmates who are very likely to return to a criminal environment. In addition, it is proposed that inmates who play a leading or coordinating role in the bicker gang environment, as the clear starting point must have attended their visits and checked their letters and telephone conversations. This will ensure that those concerned are effectively prevented from communicating about the planning or conduct of illegal activities inside or outside the prison. It is also proposed that all inmates and gang members whose grouping is actively involved in a pending violent conflict should be completely cut off from receiving visits by persons associated with the same grouping. The draft law also contains a number of penalties designed to ensure that there is a noticeable impact on biker and gang crimes. In 2016, as mentioned, there has been a marked increase in the number of shooting episodes in the public space related to rocker-gang conflicts. It poses a great risk to casual and innocent passers-by. It is therefore proposed that the penalty for shootings in public spaces be significantly increased. Specifically, the draft law provides for a new provision to be included in the criminal code, which would mean that the penalty for offences involving firearms and explosives in public spaces could be increased by up to 50%. The bill also provides for an extension of the criminal code's gang code, which tightens the penalties for biker-gang-related crime so that it can be applied in the future in more cases than it is today. The provision now applies only when the gang conflict is in progress and not, therefore, to the criminal acts leading to the conflict. That is why it is proposed to extend the gangage clause so that it also includes the crime that is suitable for bringing about a gang conflict. It can, for example, be done. be a shooting that triggers a conflict between two rival gangs about a criminal market. At the same time, it is proposed to extend the scope of the gangage provision to cover serious arms offences. The draft law also contains a proposal to tighten up the penalties for systematic and organised blackmail by one third. The sharpening will include: In the case of serious blackmail, where gangs systematically or organized collect so-called protection money from businessmen in return for leaving them alone. The draft law also proposes to relax the conditions for the abolition of a temporary ban on residence in biker fortresses and gangs. At the same time, it is proposed to double the penalty for violation of a prison ban for normally at least 60 days. With those comments, I would like to thank you for your contributions. I look forward to further consideration and, of course, I am available to answer any questions that may come here and in the further work of the committee.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509143818	Thank you, sir. Preben Bang Henriksen. And we move on to Mr. Rune Lund, Enhedslisten.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509143837	This bill implements parts of the political agreement reached between the government, the Danish People's Party and the Social Democratic Party on gang package III. The proposal provides for the possibility for municipalities to prohibit the use of a property as a place of assembly for a particular group, for example. a biker or gang group, if this group is suitable for causing, as it is called, significant inconvenience and insecurity to the surroundings. The Enhedslisten has the basic position that we should punish people for the crime they commit. And when it comes to organised criminals such as bikers and gang criminals, we have to go hard on them, because they commit serious crime, violent crime, and they have to be prosecuted, and they have to be punished for it. But we think there are a number of fundamental problems in starting to punish people, not for the crime they commit, but because they are in a certain place. We believe that it raises some fundamental problems with regard to freedom of assembly, a fundamental freedom in our democratic society, and it also raises, in fact, some fundamental problems with regard to private property and the right to use one's home, and a whole range of other issues that are relevant, a whole range of fundamental principles in our society, which are also important to defend, even when it applies to people whom, moreover, we do not understand any sympathy at all, people who commit crime, which, of course, we cannot support in any way. The package contains a proposal that it should be easier for municipalities and the police to prohibit certain people from gathering in a rocker castle, and it would be possible to prohibit certain people from entering a property if the neighbours feel bothered and if this would lead to a significant disadvantage and insecurity for the residents. The proposal is very loose and vaguely worded. Today, people can be prevented from gathering in a biker castle if there is a prospect of an attack on the house or danger to neighbours and passers-bys. This is a consequence of the bickering law that we have had since the 1990s, where the Supreme Court has also said that there is proportionality to it. The question is, is there anything about the new bill here? We feel that it is important for bikers and other organised criminals to be combated by investigation and prosecuted for their criminal acts, and at the same time, of course, we need to be better at preventing their recruitment, and we need to help those who want to leave the environment. In general terms, it is the preventive action, the rehabilitation effort, which will make a difference here on the long stretch. Over the years, in Enhedslisten, we have taken many local initiatives against bikers, and there are many ways to help fight bikers and gangs. In Kolding Enhedslisten has e.g. supported torch torch parade under the slogan "Rockers out of the city'. We have done so, while emphasising that bikers and, to that extent, gangs, like all other citizens, have constitutional rights, including freedom of association and assembly. In Copenhagen, the Enhedslisten, just as we also have in Gladsaxe, has supported the use of the planning law's possibilities to remove biker castles. And we support this kind of initiative when it is possible to do so. At the same time, we are embarrassingly aware that this is not a real solution to combat organised crime. That is the preventive action, such as the one we are discussing today. better social action, youth clubs, exit strategies and so, of course, old-fashioned police work. There are a large number of consultation responses which have also come up with their assessment of the draft law. In the opinion of the Court of Justice, the draft law's condition that the use of the property by the group as a place of assembly should be suitable to cause considerable inconvenience and insecurity to the surroundings, constitutes a very broad and discretionary formulation, in addition to which a decision is taken before a decision is taken. The National Association of Defence Attorneys notes that it is not quite clear what is meant by significant disadvantage and insecurity for the residents, and the Association considers that this should be clarified. And the Institute of Human Rights considers that the criteria that the genes of the use of the property by the group should clearly go beyond what can be expected and have been going on for a longer period of time are so central to the definition of the scope of the provision that they should appear in the legislative text, that is to say, clarification. Overall, this is a very unclear legislative proposal which risks undermining fundamental freedoms in our society. We want to fight rocker fortresses and gang crime, but not by destroying our fundamental freedoms.

We know that young people who turn out to be radicalised often find inspiration on the Internet. We have seen this in quite a number of terrorist cases both at home and abroad. With this proposal, we are introducing a new provision into the Code of Criminal Procedure which will allow websites to be blocked if it is assumed that the websites are used for specific violations of the Code of Criminal Procedure, such as propaganda for terrorist organisations. Thus, it will finally be easier to stop the spread of terrorist propaganda on the Internet. It is a proposal that we in the Social Democrats welcome and can support to such an extent. ParlaMint-DK_20170509151554	Thank you to the rapporteur. There are no brief comments, so we move on to Mr. Rune Lund, Enhedslisten.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509151607	This proposal consists of two parts and both are from the so-called anti-radicalisation package, which has been negotiated in the Ministry of Justice. And here comes the bill. First, a new provision is proposed in the Code of Procedure, which makes it possible to block a website if it is assumed that the website is used for specific criminal offences, such as propaganda for terrorist organisations. We have a number of different answers to this question, because it is always important to take a position on how to deal with something which, as such, is a serious interference in freedom of expression, that is to say, to go in and block access to certain political positions. After all, we must remember that it is, of course, illegal to call directly for participation in, for example, the situation today. violence or terror. But this is, after all, a propaganda company that you want to block access to, so to speak, some people who have often quite abhorrent attitudes, for example. Islamic State, which disseminates through the Internet their political and unsympathetic attitudes. The question then is how, in a democracy, we should deal with it, that some people have positions that we think are deeply reprehensible and that we do not sympathise with at all. Are we going to ban those positions, or are we going to have to deal with those positions in an open, democratic debate? On the side of the Enhedslisten, our position is clearly that the starting point is the last. We believe that the way in which we must combat extremism, the way in which we must fight against the unsympathetic and totalitarian attitudes, such as those of the United States and Japan. The Islamic State stands for, on, is doing so in an open, democratic debate. We also think it is important that we, as a democratic society, think about what we are doing about attitudes that we do not like. Because with legislation restricting the right of other people to spread their opinions on various political, social or international issues, instead of taking open democratic debate, we are in fact doing a service to totalitarian forces such as the Islamic State. They want to give examples to show that democracy is hypocrisy. As they say: Just see democracy It is not for you who disagree with the majority in Denmark. Look at democracy. It's only for those who think the same thing. And in this way, restrictions on democracy, restrictions on our fundamental freedoms, restrictions on freedom of expression, can be used to enable totalitarian forces to recruit people for their terrible deeds. In a number of consultation responses, concerns are expressed about the scope of the provisions to be introduced. The Institute for Human Rights has considered that the proposed provision is not necessary in addition to the existing rules and that the wordings do not contribute to clarity. And Justitia states that a general ban on terrorist propaganda hits wider than intended and that it makes it difficult for individuals to predict when the sharing of terrorist material will be punishable. The question is, then, when we share something that is propaganda and therefore criminal, so that access to it can be blocked or when we share something as part of an ordinary public democratic debate, so that we cannot see it blocked. It is therefore recommended by Justitia that the bill be limited to propaganda, Internet sites and persons associated with countries or organisations with which Denmark is in armed conflict. Compared to the proportional figure, among other things, Justitia also states that the legal basis for blocking websites poses the risk of a significant increase in the extent of Internet blocking and constitutes a restriction on the freedom of information. And the Court of Justice Association states that the proposed provision represents the possibility of extensive and serious interference with freedom of expression, and they recommend that the blocking clause should apply only to violations of Chapter 12 of the Criminal Code; it is the case of treason and other crimes against the State's independence and security; and Chapter 13 of the Criminal Code, which deals with crimes against the Constitution and the Supreme State authorities, terrorism, etc. Overall, this is something that we in the Enhedslisten regard as a serious interference in freedom of expression, which is not proportional and which risks having the opposite effect. The second part of the bill is about...

ParlaMint-DK_20170509153602	Thank you to the rapporteur. And we move on to Mr. Naser Khader, the Conservative People's Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509153610	Radicalisation and extremism are, after all, a huge challenge for our society and for free society. This must be combated partly by force and partly by means of a daily effort in Syria and Iraq, for example; that is where the inspiration comes from many times, partly by making it extremely difficult for people to be radicalised, especially the young people. They can be in different ways, including: via the Internet. We must also make it difficult to recruit young people into radicalisation, and we must make it difficult to go down and participate in activities related to extremism. And we should not use tax dollars to finance terrorist activities at all, and that is what happens when there have been examples that some people have got their services down there, which is grotesque. The aim of the draft law is to implement a number of points set out in the national action plan on preventing and combating extremism and radicalisation presented in October 2016. In other words, it is quite specifically about countering extremist propaganda and preventing online radicalisation. In future, the police will have the opportunity, following a court order, not to remove, but to block websites if violations of the criminal code's terrorist regulations are committed. The authorities can already do so in relation to Danish websites, but now it also happens in relation to websites located abroad. Then we go for public services to be stopped and repaid. As I said, Danish taxpayers should not be used to finance terrorist activities. All in all, good elements that we in the Conservative People's Party support.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509153806	Thank you to the rapporteur. Then it's the minister.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509153810	Thank you. Thank you for the predominantly positive reception of the bill. The government is concerned to ensure that we can continue to take firm action against religious radicalisation, extremism and foreign warriors. The legislative proposal before us today therefore aims to implement a number of the points set out in the national action plan on preventing and combating extremism and radicalisation, which was presented in October 2016. The action plan shall include, inter alia: A number of initiatives aimed, on the one hand, at countering extremist propaganda and preventing online radicalisation, and, on the other hand, at continuing the hard course towards foreign warriors, which will make it less attractive for residents to travel as foreign warriors. In concrete terms, therefore, the bill contains, firstly, an amendment to the Code of Criminal Procedure, so that a new provision is introduced to allow the police, following a court order, to block websites if violations of the criminal code's terrorist regulations and the criminal code's provisions on harassment and target designation of public employees are committed on the Internet on the website. The authorities can already seize Danish websites today. What the draft law is basically about at this point is ensuring that the authorities have equivalent access to take action against websites located abroad. This will ensure that we have better protection against, in particular, terrorist propaganda and terrorist acts on the Internet. Secondly, the bill contains amendments to a number of laws to ensure that the payment of public services to foreign warriors can be stopped by the authorities. The proposal also ensures that, where benefits have been paid, the authorities can recover them. The proposed amendments are therefore designed to prevent foreign warriors from financing their stay in conflict zones with public services; on the one hand, we are creating a safer and more effective procedure in cases of cessation or reimbursement of services which already have a certain possibility of involving them, and on the other hand we are creating a legal basis for stopping or refunding a number of additional services which are currently not authorised to be included at all. With those comments, I would like to thank the rapporteurs for their speeches and for their positive reception, and I look forward to the further consideration in this Chamber and also to answering questions in committee.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509180037	Thank you, sir. Klaus Markussen. There are no more brief comments. And welcome to Mr. Finn Sørensen from Enhedslisten.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509180046	Thank you. I think there is a reason to be clear about what we are actually discussing here. As the current rules, the legislation in force, are, there is no limit to the number of voluntary activities or voluntary efforts that can be committed to as a citizen of this country, even for unemployment benefits and survivors. The Minister himself writes this in the comments to the bill. This means that unemployed people and pensioners can act as visiting friends, they can gather to relief organisations, they can operate in recycling shops, they can participate in sports activities, including as trainers in sports associations, where it is not usual to hire paid trainers, and many, many other voluntary activities, which hundreds of thousands of citizens participate in every day, fortunately, and a huge thanks to the citizens for that, because this kind of volunteering is a really strong and positive contribution to society. That's just not what this bill is about. That is not what we are discussing. We are discussing the extent to which benefits and survivors may take part in voluntary unpaid work without being offset in cash benefits and early retirement. And I admit to the puzzling listeners that one can ask: what is the difference now? Well, the difference is that voluntary unpaid work is something quite different from voluntary work and voluntary activity. Voluntary unpaid work is, according to the definitions of the law, as the Minister correctly describes it, a work which can be offered as wage work and which usually will be. This means that ordinary wage work must be paid in accordance with normal, usual Danish wages and working conditions. Here, then, the law itself states that beneficiaries of benefits can do this kind of voluntary unpaid work for up to 4 hours without being deducted from their benefit, while the experimental scheme has increased this limit to 15 hours. It is then made clear that, in the case of actual operational tasks, this leads to a set-off in cash benefits and early retirement from the first hour. In principle, it is a really bad idea if the unemployed in general are or are not insured and retired, who want to supplement the early retirement with some work and can do it according to the law, are doing voluntary unpaid work. For that simply means that they are cheating themselves for an ordinary job at normal pay. They take an ordinary job from themselves. The law then says that you must do unpaid work for up to 4 hours. We really think that's wrong, but let's do it. I think we're down to a small limit. We were and are opposed to setting the limit and are therefore also opposed to a limit of 15 hours, experiment or not. Then you might ask: What damage has it done? There is only one study, and it is, moreover, a very thin basis to extend a law on the basis of which it is Deloitte's investigation. It is true that it has been done on a slim basis. It shows that there is limited displacement of ordinary jobs, and it uses some then as an argument for there to be no problems. Yes, but the reason why it is a limited displacement is that the scheme has been used to a very limited extent. Then it is clear that the displacement remains in the same proportions. Nevertheless, the report gives a fairly clear indication that it actually leads to the displacement of ordinary jobs, since a quarter of those who have been interviewed indicate that the task they performed would be carried out by paid labour if it had not been carried out by a volunteer and that almost one in five consider that the performance of their task requires or requires special training, which also suggests that it is a quite ordinary job. I'm not surprised the government would like to prolong the experiment. Nor am I surprised that yesterday the government came up with a major proposal for an additional opportunity to work free of charge in functions which should be paid under normal conditions. Unfortunately, this is entirely in line with what changing governments and a lot of municipalities are trying to do in these years, namely to replace employees in ordinary jobs with volunteers, with people in business practices, useful jobs and other kinds of cheap or free labour. We are very much in agreement with the position of the LO and the FTF on this matter. We voted against the experimental scheme and therefore also voted against its extension.

Stress has gone on and become the new people's disease, and it is high time that a comprehensive coordinated work was set in motion. 35,000 health reports every day are due to psychological circumstances, many in the workplace have a backache, and a lot of people have a management problem. Half of the country's managers with staff responsibilities are not trained in dealing with stress and mental health and safety. If we are to do something about public disease stress, we need to do something about it. a requirement is introduced for all managers to undertake a mental health and safety education. We need to put the psychological working environment on an equal footing with the physical one. The president of the FTF, Bente Sorgenfrey, said recently: "One way for jobs to really equalise physical and psychological working conditions is for managers to see the area. They must have knowledge and concrete tools to ensure a good psychological working environment in the workplace. There must be a requirement for all managers with staff responsibilities to receive in-service training in the mental health and safety environment within the first year. The focus must be on prevention, retention and knowledge of factors that promote a good mental health and safety environment, and training can be provided by a number of approved public and private providers, just as we see from the current mandatory OSH training. It may be easier to see the physical damage, but in fact the Institute for Public Health has estimated that poor mental health and safety at work contributes to 1,400 deaths per year. Barely 15%. of those employed have symptoms of stress or depression due to bullying, violence, threats of violence or psychosocial influences in the workplace. At the same time, it is estimated that one out of every five long-term health reports is due to poor mental health and safety conditions. The FTF has calculated that poor mental health and safety at work costs in the region of 32 million. kr. annual because of sickness absence, early retirement and health care costs. A study by Epinion on behalf of the LO and the FTF shows that in the past 12 months, one in three employees has experienced sickness absenteeism on the job, which could have been avoided if management had had better skills. Let us be inspired by among others. Sweden and give the psychological working environment something as simple as its own announcement. The President of the 3F, Per Christensen, recently said: "Stress must never become the individual employee's problem. Studies show that poor management is the main obstacle to employees being able to avoid stress and poor working conditions. In the organisation Leaders, who organize more than 100,000 managers, are also in favour of more people being trained in mental health and stress management, although they will not make it mandatory. We need to rethink the whole work environment. SF proposes six concrete bets. We propose a national action plan against stress; that stress clinics be set up that can quickly provide free help; that we get a new hotline for poor mental health and safety; that there be better opportunities for recognising a psychological injury; that the Factory Inspectorate gets a boost; and that managers have a psychological work environment on the school schedule.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509193819	Thank you. First of all, I would like to thank the Prime Minister for using this evening. ParlaMint-DK_20170509193917	Thank you. Then it is the Prime Minister for answering the question. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509193926	Thank you so much. Of course, I would like to spend an evening discussing the role of the Ministry of Finance, so what else would I get the evening to do? So I've been looking forward to it a lot. And the questioners have asked me to assess whether it is appropriate for the Ministry of Finance to have as much influence as it has on all policy areas. Let me begin by expressing my support for this question, because, as stated in the government base, the government's vision is a Denmark of growth, because growth and prosperity are the prerequisites for welfare. At the same time, it is very important for the government to pursue a responsible economic policy. This is because the economy has filled some of the overall picture on top of some challenging years in Europe and in the world in general. We have also seen this in Denmark, and this has of course affected the political priorities. We have constantly had to make difficult decisions that have been necessary to ensure the resilience of the Danish economy. We can then rejoice that the Danish economy is in very good shape. This is not least due to the responsible economic policy of this government, the former Left Government and former VK governments, and here the Ministry of Finance plays an absolutely natural and central role in the conduct of the government's economic policy. This is true under the present government as well as under previous governments. This is due to the simple fact that it is in this way the task of the Ministry of Finance. So yes, the Ministry of Finance is central to my government and has been it for my former governments. This is one of the reasons why things are actually going well in Denmark. It is the case that the constitution is based on the arrangement that the government is set up as a ministerial system. And the ministerial system basically means that each minister, as head of administration, has an independent competence and responsibility within his administration area. This also means that political decisions are taken by the minister responsible in the individual areas and, in fact, not by the government as a college. As a picture of the ministerial system, it has been stated in the literature of State law that the executive consists of a number of co-ordinated pyramids, each with its own minister at the head. And on the occasion of this question about the role and influence of the Ministry of Finance on other ministries, it makes a little sense to explain these things. I'll be happy to do that when I'm asked. But I would first like to make it clear that, in line with previous governments on principle, I do not wish to comment on issues relating to purely intergovernmental relations, and I also had to understand that there was respect about that. Thank you. For how the government organises its work internally and who is involved in what and when is a matter for the government. I shall not go into that further today. But, in other words, the rule of the constitution is that it is the Prime Minister who appoints the ministers and that the government is made up of the ministers appointed by the Prime Minister. This does not mean that the Prime Minister is completely free to appoint ministers. After all, it also follows from the Constitution that no government can be constitutionally appointed which is likely to be faced with a motion of censure in the Folketing. On the other hand, there is no need for support. That is what is called negative parliamentarianism. The Constitution does not contain rules which specify the number of ministers or the fields of work of individual ministers, also known as resort. On the other hand, it is up to the Prime Minister, under the Constitution, to determine the number of ministers and the distribution of administrative areas between them. And since the Prime Minister's competence directly follows from the Constitution, the legislature, the Folketing, cannot with binding effect determine the number of ministers or the distribution of administrative areas between ministers. So it is up to the Prime Minister in office at any time, under the Constitution, to decide which responsibility should be the responsibility of each minister, including the Minister of Finance. Of course, this also has an impact on which cases, for example. The Minister of Finance is involved in this. And, as I have said, it is, in fact, the task of the Minister of Finance to be involved in matters of government policy. However, it is also important for me to stress that this is a natural consequence of the distribution of competences which the ministerial system expresses that political decisions are taken not by the government as a college but by the minister who has been given responsibility for a given matter. Moreover, it is a natural consequence that there is no minister with overall competence in relation to others. So the premise in the question, that is, that the Ministry of Finance should have an influence on all areas of the resort, is therefore not correct. It is both formal and indeed the case that the Ministry of Finance has its seat, as do other ministries. Let me also make it clear that it is not the ministries as authorities but the ministers who take the political decisions. They are, of course, based on the professional advice which ministers receive from the government departments. If a majority of the Folketing is opposed to the way in which a given minister manages his seat, the rule of the Constitution is that the Folketing has the power to exercise political responsibility vis-à-vis the Minister. Thus, under the Constitution, a minister cannot continue in his duties if a majority in the Folketing has expressed distrust of him or her. However, when it is important to make it clear that each minister is responsible for his or her area within the ministerial system, it is not least because there is also a legal dimension to the responsibility of ministers, which also arises from the constitution. Each Minister shall be responsible for his/her territory and for his/her territory only. See, my intention in this account of the constitutional framework of the ministerial system, including the political and legal responsibilities associated with it, is not to give the impression that there is no government coordination between ministries and between ministers, because it is an entirely natural part of sitting in government. This applies to the present one and, as I think it was also sought by the previous one. Of course, coordination is natural when a government is made up of several parties which, of course, will not always agree on all the details. But it has to be done, for example. is also coordinated when the government's policy on matters relating to the responsibilities of several ministers is to be determined. Often it will be the case that policy in the fields of other ministers goes beyond policy in the field of finance, because there is an economic implication. That is why the Minister of Finance will typically be involved in this coordination, so it is. In this way there are some ministers in the government who have some cross-cutting tasks, not that it makes them superior, but they have cross-cutting tasks. This applies in addition to the Minister for Finance, for example. also the Minister of Justice and the Minister of Foreign Affairs. The ongoing coordination takes place particularly in the context of ministerial meetings and the various intergovernmental committees set up, particularly the government's coordination committee and the government's economic committee. These committees of government are, moreover, quite broadly composed. The intergovernmental coordination that is taking place does not shake the fact that we in Denmark have a ministerial system, as I have already explained. I hope that this explanation of the constitutional framework of the ministerial system has helped to create a greater degree of clarity so that we can point out the myth that has emerged that the Ministry of Finance should have a special influence on other ministerial areas. As I said, that is not the case. Thank you for the floor.

I think it is a pity that sometimes a minister is tied on his hands and feet, or when there are areas where one can look at a trade ministry, that if they and the investments are made within the Ministry's own financial framework, then perhaps money will be saved in the long term. It will be an investment, but it cannot be done here and now because of the governance that exists. So that is actually the reason for today's debate, and I would like to read out a proposal for adoption on behalf of the Enhedslisten, the Radical Left, the Alternative and the SF: Proposals for adoption The Parliament notes that it is the responsibility of the current government to organise the work of the government and ministries at all times. The Danish Parliament notes that in recent years there has been a considerable centralisation of political decisions, particularly in the Ministry of Finance, rather than in the individual ministries of resort, and that this challenges both the development of political solutions and the democratic legitimacy of decisions. Parliament calls on the government to ensure greater leeway for political development between the ministries of the Council, the Folketing and the citizens in respect of the financial framework. • V 101).

ParlaMint-DK_20170509203513	Thank you. No one else has any comments. Then it's Mrs. Josephine Fock, the alternative.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509203525	Thank you for the floor. Thanks to the SF for calling this debate. There should be no doubt that the alternative wants sustainability on all three bottom lines, both economic, social and environmental. In Denmark, after all, the Ministry of Finance is primarily responsible for the economic bottom line, and that is not a problem to that extent. However, the way in which the Ministry of Finance has secured its influence and the way in which the power of the Ministry of Finance has developed, in our optics, causes two major problems. The narrow economic thinking in the Ministry of Finance means that long-term investment is not being made in people and the environment, and, as we see it, the Ministry of Finance has also begun to dictate how priority should be given to both the social and the environmental bottom line. Overall, it can be said that there is too little investment in people and the environment. It is clear that the task of the Ministry of Finance is to ensure that there is a balance on the economic bottom line, and this is ensured by means of the models that we know, with names such as the legal model, ADAM etc. There is no doubt that economic models can be fine, because we need to, like trying to predict what will happen if we do so and so, but the models we currently have are based on assumptions which, even within economic logic, are very simplistic. It is simply assumed that the citizens behave as perfect machines that can predict everything and thus allow the models in reality only to invest in material things, but not in people. You invest in bricks and motorways, but not in the vulnerable and in education. It is therefore gratifying that the Ministry of Finance has set up a working group to look at the assumption that people are not perfect machines. That is a good thing, but the problem with the working group that has been set up is that it barely scratches the surface of the problems that exist in the economic calculation models. It will not look at the negative effects of poverty; it will not look at the positive effects of education; it will not look at the consequences of pollution destroying our climate and the environment. So until we have a proper reckoning with the arithmetic models, the logic of the Ministry of Finance helps us invest too much in bricks and too little in people, that we invest too much in motorways and too little in the environment, and that we invest too much in the present and too little in the future. After all, the logic of the Ministry of Finance also dictates priority on the social and environmental bottom line. The economic thinking that prevails in the Ministry of Finance has been called both neoliberal and neoclassical, or the word 'competition state' has been used. But no matter what you call it, it is central that it is based on the fact that people are selfish and focus only on their own consumption. This forgot that people are moral and social beings who care about others and want to work. The problem is that if you think only of people as selfish and self-absorbed, as the Ministry of Finance and through the arithmetic models, then the logical answer is the competitive state. We do not trust the unemployed to want to work. It is ensured by means of financial incentives and by means of meaningless activation processes and meaningless application requirements to say that it must be as intolerable as possible to be available. And because we do not trust that these are real refugees, we use a very, very harsh rhetoric and introduce low benefits instead of actually doing the opposite: to say that once they have been granted refugee status, we must, of course, welcome them. And because you do not trust that public servants want to do their job as well as possible, you choose to control them and thus work on welfare with documentation. Similarly, the logic of the Ministry of Finance has also begun to dictate environmental policy, so that the economic bottom line is placed above the environmental bottom line, and it is at the expense of the disappearance of the PSP. Here the principle of the polluter having to pay the most for the green conversion was abolished. At that cost, an agreement has been reached on electric cars which have completely stalled the electricity distribution system and, at that cost, a new agreement has also been reached on the North Sea, which has decided to pull even more oil out of the underground, although we know that 80%. In fact, the world's fossil reserves must remain in the ground if we are to remain within the 2-degree objective to which Denmark has committed itself in the Paris Agreement. It is, in fact, also with the prospect of a highly questionable revenue based on dubious predictions about oil prices. In the alternative, we would like to have a much broader approach to the economy and society, which is why we say that we need to think in three bottom lines and not one. And those who work with the economic bottom line must stop seeing the social bottom line and the environmental bottom line as opponents and instead start seeing them as fellow players. That is why we are saying that the Ministry of Finance should not go into the territory of other ministries. Rather, it is the areas and bottom lines of the other ministries that need to be considered in the Ministry of Finance, and in this way we believe that we can take an important step towards a more empathetic society, a more forward-looking society, and that we move from being a competitive state to becoming a balance society.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509210816	The debate is open, and that is first Mr President. Jesper Petersen, Social Democratic Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20170509210855	Thank you. The massive problems and scandals of recent years have revealed that there are deep problems with the SKAT. This has weakened the confidence of citizens and companies in the functioning of our tax administration, and this is deeply problematic for a tax-financed welfare society such as the Danish one. It is a task of duty that must and must be rectified. One of the major problems that has been in our tax system for a number of years is the problems of faulty property assessments, in fact so problematic that the previous government had to decide back in 2013 to suspend the system and continue the assessments until a new system was in place. Fortunately, we have now reached the stage of examining the legislation implementing the agreement on a new system of property valuation. The property tax is a significant source of tax, which is worth some 40 billion. kr. per year in tax revenue to drive our welfare community for. For the individual it may also be a significant amount paid in tax. It is quite untenable that we have been in a situation where the basis for the tax payment has been as imprecise as it was and where it has not been possible to complain about the property assessment, and it is in some way rather odd that people in Denmark seem to have just got used to it being so, although it is a very big problem and not something that belongs in an orderly society like ours. So I welcome among many others, I believe, that in November 2016, a broad majority of Parliament's parties concluded the agreement for a new and far more accurate and transparent property assessment system. A major damage to the base of our tax system is now being repaired before it goes completely wrong, for the benefit of anyone who somehow pays property tax, and that is of course the vast majority of us, and for the benefit of legal certainty in the field of taxation. On top of that, a new housing tax is applied, which, as you know, has been agreed a week ago. We are not going to deal with this today, but it is part of the picture of the creation of orderly conditions, predictability and security of property taxation and the economy of property owners. We are pleased about this as Social Democrats. The scope of the legislative proposals reflects the fact that we are working on a larger machine here, but together they create the regulatory framework for the further development of the new assessment system, which will become more reliable, transparent and fair. So some impact points in the proposals start with safety. The assessment of houses and properties is not an exact science. There will always be an element of discretion, in relation to what is exactly the right price for a house or property, and once things are running on the market and a seller has to find a buyer, there can be all sorts of conditions that apply, which means that a particular house or apartment is sold a little differently than you might think. So there is not an exact price in this way, but with the new system based on actual trade prices in an area, there is a significant improvement in safety. An assessment standard is introduced, an uncertainty margin of +/- 20%, within which the assessment must be carried out. The owner is given the opportunity to provide additional information about the property, which will contribute to the accuracy of the assessment. The proposal also lays the foundations for more uniform assessments. The new system will provide a much greater uniformity and ensure that property owners gain a better insight into how the valuation of their property has been made. To this end, a declaration procedure is introduced so that you can see what information is important for the assessment of your property and, as something new, you will also be able to supplement it with new information before the assessment is carried out. In this way, the assessment of a house should in future be justified and explained to the individual owner and be significantly more transparent than it is today, and that is also positive. The proposal also addresses one of the obvious problems left behind by the old system, namely that there are homeowners who have paid too much tax, because the assessments have been wrong and have therefore been continued for a number of years. With the proposal here, those who have paid tax on too high an estimate will automatically pay back the too much paid tax with a percentage surcharge, and that is only fair. Not least at the request of the Social Democratic Party, and other parties too have been concerned about this, it has also been decided to set up a refund scheme for tenants and shareholders, as exactly as is now possible. Repayments will typically be made in rural areas and in smaller towns where assessments have been too high. In relation to agriculture, among other things: There is, of course, a simplification which lies in the draft law, where you can make a projection of the value of the reasons. In the consultation phase, an additional element has been added, as the government has requested, to an additional 5-percent reduction in the tax base. This is an element that we would like to examine more closely in the committee procedure, which was not part of the negotiations in the first place. The legislative proposals also propose new rules for the review of complaints. In future, complaints about assessment cases will be dealt with mainly by a number of new assessment boards, which must be anchored locally and have a high level of professional competence. Here it has been important for us to maintain a layman element, and there is still a discussion about exactly what the decree issued by the Minister to say what the new boards should be structured looks like, and so, as I said, it is important for us to maintain a layman element and an understanding of local conditions. It is also established that it costs 1,000 DKK. to complain about its property assessment, and here I think it is important to say that there is then a declaration procedure, a greater transparency in how to achieve the assessment that has been made, greater reliability in the system in general, a 20% transparency principle and, hopefully, a less need to complain. This is, of course, one of the concerns one can have, i.e. whether there is dimensioning to deal with the complaints that will be, but I think it has to be said that we will have a system that is significantly more rightful and that greater transparency has been created as a citizen in order for there to be a lower level of complaint. Nevertheless, there is a task in ensuring that citizens trust it and that the system works so well that it can be trusted. In addition, initiatives are proposed to improve the quality of the handling of complaints and at the same time make the treatment more effective. The new system will be used for the first time for the assessment of owner-occupied dwellings in 2018 and for the assessment of other properties, including commercial properties and also non-profit housing and cooperative housing, in 2019. With the legislative proposals, we will ensure the legislative framework needed to establish an assessment system that will provide more accurate assessments than it has been in the past and will thus be a solid basis for collecting the EUR 42 billion or so. DKK, which citizens and companies pay annually in real estate taxes. It has to be said here that this is a real and important problem which the Danish Parliament will solve during this parliamentary term. As I have said, therefore, it has been quite untenable that the valuation of real estate was so incorrect and had to be suspended altogether since then. It is only good and fair that we now have a system that is more to be trusted. As we can see, we are in favour of the proposals and will vote in favour of both the legislative proposals we are working together here.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509211618	I shall start with the history of the fact that we are here today, because it is actually a matter that has been going on for a very long time, not the legislative proposal itself, but the problem. This means that you have a house, for example. is estimated at 1 million. kr., you pay property tax of 20%. less than that, that's 800,000 kroner. That is also reasonable, because the uncertainty that exists in the fact that the assessment does not hit precisely must of course benefit the citizen. I think that is actually very good, both to give a better insight into how the authority has come up with an assessment, but also because it can take the top of the need to complain, that you can report early in the process if there are some things that may be factually wrong, or if your house might be close to a windmill or other things that you don't know about can influence the price you can get for the house, and hence also the assessment. One of the things I think is gonna be crucial in this process is that it's gonna be easy to understand. I think most homeowners, including myself, cannot find a head and tail in how to come up with the assessment that is today. In other words, it is simply impossible for an ordinary Dane to see through it, and I think it is crucial that the new system should be able to see if it can be seen, for example. is a neighboring sale that has formed the basis, or that is how far it is estimated that the house is from water, or how large it is, so it is obvious which parameters are used. This is simply crucial, so that one can also be confident that the right data has been used to come to the most qualified guess, one can almost say the valuation of the property, because it never hits a hundred percent. I think that is absolutely crucial. Having said that, however, it is clear that there will also be some who do not feel they are getting the right assessment and still need to complain. So a good right of appeal is, of course, absolutely crucial in terms of legal certainty. And at least there I think it has been important that we have tried to keep the complaint fee down. It is down to about DKK 1,000, which is of course to be compared with the average cost of DKK 8,500. to deal with a complaint. And as has been said, of course, you get the 1,000 kroner. If you win the complaint, you get back. After all, we are dealing with two legislative proposals, and the second legislative proposal, that is, L 212, is the proposal which mostly deals with the right of appeal. I think that's super good. This can help to increase professionalism. There have been some figures in the past that of the cases decided by the Board of Appeal, 73 per cent were reversed when they were transferred to the National Tax Court. This might suggest that there was a need for a strengthening of professionalism. But we think it is important that there is still some sort of layman structure over it, so there is some local knowledge which is also crucial, i.e. if it is to be done, for example. is a real estate agent who is put in as a professional, that he is then himself from the area where he has to evaluate properties. So it is important, as Jesper Petersen also said in his rapporteur's speech, that we ensure both local representation and division in circles. How big do they have to be? We also think that this is worth a closer look at. Finally, I would like to conclude by saying that there is one small thing that is also included in the draft law, which I think is worth noting: there has been the challenge in the past that when a municipality changed a local plan, that is, the basis for what the percentage of settlements, for example. must be in an area, or when an area was laid out for center purposes, then a reason became much more valuable. And then the homeowner was faced with the problem of getting a much higher ground debt without necessarily being able to do anything about it. Because it could be, for example, that you... After 10 years of having had a higher ground debt, find that there should then not be the second use of the land and then the local plan will be changed back. I mean, you may never have had a chance to gain a profit from the fact that there was a different level of use. This bill introduces a "can' clause so that the municipalities can exempt an owner from this increase in the ground debt while a change in the planning basis is being made. We think this is quite reasonable, because you have bought a house on the assumption that you can sit there for many years. And the fact that some people come from outside with a modified local plan cannot be held responsible for that property owner. In conclusion, we are part of the agreement that has been made, both on the assessments, but also on the second part of taxation, which will come later. ParlaMint-DK_20170509212830	After all, these two legislative proposals contain different elements, and I will choose to focus on the things that make us critical of the legislative proposals, which, of course, must also be seen in the context of the housing tax agreement that has been concluded here last week. Especially in relation to the assessment standard of plus/minus 20%, which then has to reflect the uncertainty in the assessments, we have the view that we think it is too much. Our basic position is that instead of creating and operating with a lot of uncertainty margins, we should create an assessment system that is, from the outset, accurate and that, in addition, we can have a very good right of appeal. That will be the best solution for us to see. Then we think that another problem with this proposal is that for agricultural and forestry buildings it is proposed to continue the current assessments under the so-called farmer's rule in a new projection scheme. In practice, the farmer's rule means that agricultural land is assessed at a much lower value than the actual value of the land. This is not primarily due to the farmer's rule in itself, which, apart from the wording of the medium-sized farm, is based essentially on having to hit current trade prices. On the other hand, the low estimates are due to the fact that since 1992 recruitments under the farmer's rule have been carried out in the form of projections of the per hectare price. And those projections have been very cautious, and the result is therefore that the estimates under the farmer's rule are only about a third of the price that can be found to be traded for when agricultural land is traded in free trade. We have asked from the Enhedslisten in the past what the proceeds would be if, as it were, the farmer's rule was abolished, and we are talking about an amount of about 1 billion. kr. a year, which could be used to finance welfare initiatives. In addition, it is proposed that repayment schemes be set up for owner-occupied dwellings and businesses to ensure that property owners who have paid tax after too high an estimate are automatically reimbursed the tax. And, of course, it is perfectly logical that, if you have paid too much tax on a wrong basis, you should have your money back. So that part of the proposal is, of course, entirely logical and it can be supported by Enhedslisten. Overall, we would like to see a well-functioning property tax system, but to introduce a 20% uncertainty as a standard, we do not think that is satisfactory. In addition, we are quite unhappy that the farmer-farming rule is being maintained, which is a totally unfair tax concession. Then we have a large number of technical questions about the way in which the assessments are to be calculated, so in this context there are some questions on my part in connection with the work of the committee. And then we will have the opportunity later to discuss the housing tax agreement itself and the imbalance that is inherent in it, in relation to among others. the tax rebate introduced with the 20% precautionary principle, which means that one million villa north of Copenhagen to 15 million. kr. will receive a deduction of 3 million. kr., while a house in Næstved to 1 million. kr. e.g. will only receive a deduction of DKK 200,000. So it is a housing tax agreement, which we would rather have drawn up in a completely different way, namely by simply phasing out the tax freeze, both the tax freeze on the property value tax and the increase in the land tax. We have, of course, been given figures in the past that the increase in the land tax and the tax freeze on the property value tax total amount to a price of 11 billion. kr. in 2015. And it is clear that if you phased out that tax freeze, you would free billions of dollars for welfare. At the same time, it could be combined with a freezing system which has been proposed, which will ensure that as a homeowner you will not be able to be taxed out of your home because of current taxation. So the combination of a phasing-out and a freezing system could solve many problems. It would solve three key issues for the Enhedslisten: 1) that we get a housing tax system without price bubble; 2) that we get a fair tax on socially created values; and 3) that we have a housing tax system in which we minimise the risk of people being taxed out of their homes because of a current housing tax. So the Enhedslisten is negatively disposed towards the two proposals.

I think they deserve a thank you in this context. However, as I said, it is complicated and difficult to make a public property assessment system because it is basically about guessing the market price of a property. After all, the market price of the properties is dependent on a wide range of factors, including something as simple as the seller's situation, local conditions, economic conditions in general. It's not uncomplicated. When it has been decided politically that citizens should be taxed on the basis of the value of their housing, it is, of course, fair that citizens can expect the public property assessment to hit as precisely as possible. Taking away citizens' money is one of the most intrusive things that we politicians can do. So citizens must be able to have confidence in the property assessment and that any complaints about the assessment are dealt with seriously and professionally and quickly. Citizens must also be able to see why their accommodation has been assessed as it is. That is fair because, as I said, the assessment is of great importance to the citizens' economy. Doubts must also benefit citizens, which is why it is also difficult to see this bill, regardless of the agreement we have just concluded, on property taxation itself, in which the tax base itself represents the public property assessment minus 20%. Public property assessments have been suspended since 2013 and this is an indication that the public property assessment system has actually collapsed. It is in itself a remarkable situation in an otherwise highly developed society as the Danish one. But now we will have a new property assessment system and we will have a new complaint system. I also think we should be honest and say that it is probably not possible to create an entirely perfect property assessment system. There will be advantages and there will be disadvantages. In our view, the new system is not perfect either. There are various considerations which need to be weighed against each other. I think we should be honest about that. There will probably also continue to be citizens who will wonder at the valuation of their housing, especially if they are right that their housing is overrated but will not change the public property assessment because it is within the uncertainty range of plus/minus 20%. But that is an indication that the public property assessment is an estimate. The market price of the same house may vary from week to week and even between relatively similar houses. Having said that, however, we believe that the new property assessment system has created the best possible property assessment system, and I also believe that it can be said with certainty that it will be even one of the best in perhaps the whole world. We can be proud of that. I certainly hope so. However, I am not so naive that I believe that we will not experience problems in the future or that the discussion will stop when it is implemented. We will probably continue to have to discuss this and will probably continue to work on it. But the overall framework is now firmly established, and we are pleased about that. So, of course, we support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-DK_20170509214555	A few years ago, the National Audit Office came up with a devastating criticism of the assessment system; that is no exaggeration.
