ParlaMint-DK_20170509215244	Thank you for the floor, President. After all, it is a pity and a pity that such a bill should be dealt with on Tuesday evening, because it is an historic agreement that has been made on the property tax, and it is an historic agreement that has been made on property valuations. These are two agreements which mean that the justification for levying property taxes as the source of income which property taxes are for the state, so that we can use the money for our welfare society, will be fair and fair to the country's homeowners. I choose not to take the late hour we are discussing this draft law as an indication of how it will be prioritised, and I would like instead to welcome the positive reports that have come from the rapporteurs who have been on the platform. There has been a predominantly positive reception, not only from the parties involved in the agreement, but also from a number of other parties which are not involved in the settlement. Restoring confidence in public property assessments is an essential task for society. Property owners must have confidence in the property valuations that are being made and they must have access to complain under proper and reasonable conditions, because we owe this to the property owners in relation to levying property taxes on a legitimate basis. In my view, as a tax minister, we simply cannot be aware to go any further with a system where there is so much uncertainty about what is the starting point for taxation, that is, the housing tax. We feel that, when we tax wages, people know what they are paid, and we also have a fairly square, schematic system of what you then have to pay at different tax rates, depending on what you get in pay. That is to say, it is right and fair to all. Then tax rates can be discussed, that is fair enough, but the basis for the collection of income tax and personal taxes is in order. When you tax something on an ongoing basis, you can discuss the fairness and fairness of it, and when you tax something on the basis of an assessment, you can in particular problematize it, and when we now find ourselves in a situation where we know that the property assessments we have today are not good enough and do not rely on a fair enough basis, we, as responsible politicians, naturally owe it to ourselves to come together and find a better system to assess what to pay in housing tax. And that is what we have done together. It is a project that actually started under the former government, then the tax minister, Mr. Benny Engelbrecht set up the Engberg Committee to look at a model for this. The committee did not come up with a large, chrome-plated solution, but it did come up with a good, professionally sound starting point so that we could continue to work towards a new system that assessed the homes more correctly and more fairly. We have then worked on this in my year and a half as tax minister, and it ended up in the agreement on a new property assessment system, which a broad majority in the Folketing is behind, and that agreement was reached before Christmas. The basis for restoring the confidence that has suffered a break is secured by these two draft laws. In any case, implementation and implementation of the two legislative proposals are ensured. In the future, property assessments can be carried out on a much better basis and with a higher degree of certainty, while at the same time the property owners are involved in a kind of declaration process where they gain insight into the basis of the assessments. You simply get a proposal for an assessment message through your letter slot or in your e-mailbox, and there you have the opportunity to look at it and look at what is behind the assessment that SKAT has made, challenge it and declare the things that the tax administration and the assessment authority may not have taken on board. At the same time, property owners will be given the opportunity to provide this additional information. Hopefully, this will lead to a more transparent and more accurate assessment system than what we have today. At the same time, the legislative proposals also contain a refund scheme, which is historic, so that the property owners who have paid tax on too high an assessment will automatically get the taxes back without having to complain. That's 9 billion. kr., we send back to ordinary Danes, typically those who live in the smaller house outside the larger cities, who have paid too much tax because they have paid tax on a wrong and too high rating. It is only right and proper that they should get the money back. This falls outside the scope of the draft legislation under discussion, but I would also like to make a few brief comments on the new housing tax system, on which the same group of parties have agreed. It is about the Danes getting security not only in relation to the assessment, but also in relation to the taxation. I would like to thank you for the cooperation we have had on the assessment and in relation to taxation. It has been a long process across changing governments. It was first the narrow Left Government and then the new, larger and better government and the process that followed it. We have had some good long discussions where we have come to the bottom of both the assessment and taxation side and have reached each other in what I think is a good and sensible compromise in which all the participating parties can see themselves, and which, in these times when the people's regime is challenged around the world, shows that in Denmark, when it comes to important things, we can find a way of working together for the good of the community and for Denmark, and therefore for something that at least a broad circle of parties can see themselves in. I think that is actually quite remarkable. At any rate, this is something that I, as Minister of Taxation, am proud of. I am, of course, available if there are any questions in this Chamber today. Otherwise, there is a committee procedure that we have to do before the bill is adopted before Parliament goes on summer vacation. I would like to conclude by saying, although it is obviously not so nice when I understand that the rapporteur for the Enhedslisten cannot be present in the Chamber any longer, that I have a little difficulty in understanding that one can be opposed to it. I understand that we may be opposed to the taxation system we are proposing, but that is not what we are going to vote on here. Basically, however, I do not understand why we can be opposed to a system in which we have more right and correct assessments. And that's what we get. In my view, it is absolutely untenable to continue with the system we have. I would therefore urge those who are sceptical to think carefully before they choose to vote red on this proposal. You can then choose to vote red on the tax part when we have to legislate on it, as we approach 2020 and 2021, but a new and better assessment system, in which we ensure a more proper basis for what is to be taxed, is therefore difficult to oppose.
